=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Alice and Bob are playing a fantasy battle game consisting of n rounds where they summon one of three magical creatures each round: a Fire Dragon, a Water Serpent, or an Earth Golem. In each round, players simultaneously summon their creature and are awarded points as follows:

If one player summons a Fire Dragon and the other summons an Earth Golem, the player who summoned the Fire Dragon is awarded a point.
If one player summons a Water Serpent and the other summons a Fire Dragon, the player who summoned the Water Serpent is awarded a point.
If one player summons an Earth Golem and the other summons a Water Serpent, the player who summoned the Earth Golem is awarded a point.
If both players summon the same creature, no player is awarded a point.

You are given a string s consisting of n characters 'F', 'W', and 'E', representing the sequence of creatures Alice will summon in each round:

If s[i] == 'F', Alice summons a Fire Dragon.
If s[i] == 'W', Alice summons a Water Serpent.
If s[i] == 'E', Alice summons an Earth Golem.

Bob’s sequence of moves is unknown, but it is guaranteed that Bob will never summon the same creature in two consecutive rounds. Bob beats Alice if the total number of points awarded to Bob after n rounds is strictly greater than the points awarded to Alice.
Return the number of distinct sequences Bob can use to beat Alice.
Since the answer may be very large, return it modulo 10^9 + 7.
 
Example 1:

Input: s = "FFF"
Output: 3
Explanation:
Bob can beat Alice by making one of the following sequences of moves: "WFW", "FWF", or "WEW". Note that other winning sequences like "WWE" or "EWW" are invalid since Bob cannot make the same move twice in a row.

Example 2:

Input: s = "FWEFW"
Output: 18
Explanation:
Bob can beat Alice by making one of the following sequences of moves: "FWFWF", "FWFWE", "FWEFE", "FWEWE", "FEFWF", "FEFWE", "FEFEW", "FEWFE", "WFEFE", "WFEWE", "WEFWF", "WEFWE", "WEFEF", "WEFEW", "WEWFW", "WEWFE", "EWFWE", or "EWEWE".

 
Constraints:

1 <= s.length <= 1000
s[i] is one of 'F', 'W', or 'E'.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        
```

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Implementation details:

MOD = 10**9+7
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        n = len(s)
        offset = n
        M = 2*n + 1  # scores -n..n
        # map char to 0,1,2
        mp = {'F':0,'W':1,'E':2}
        # dp[last][score_index] maybe list of 3 lists length M
        dp = [[0]*M for _ in range(3)]
        a0 = mp[s[0]]
        for b in range(3):
            if b == (a0 + 1) % 3:
                inc = 1
            elif b == (a0 - 1) % 3:
                inc = -1
            else:
                inc = 0
            dp[b][offset + inc] = 1
        for ch in s[1:]:
            a = mp[ch]
            ndp = [[0]*M for _ in range(3)]
            for last in range(3):
                row = dp[last]
                # maybe if row all zeros skip
                for b in range(3):
                    if b == last:
                        continue
                    if b == (a + 1) % 3:
                        inc = 1
                    elif b == (a - 1) % 3:
                        inc = -1
                    else:
                        inc = 0
                    # update
                    if inc == 0:
                        # shift none
                        for idx, val in enumerate(row):
                            if val:
                                ndp[b][idx] = (ndp[b][idx] + val) % MOD
                    elif inc == 1:
                        for idx in range(M-1):
                            val = row[idx]
                            if val:
                                ndp[b][idx+1] = (ndp[b][idx+1] + val) % MOD
                    else:
                        for idx in range(1,M):
                            val = row[idx]
                            if val:
                                ndp[b][idx-1] = (ndp[b][idx-1] + val) % MOD
            dp = ndp
        ans = 0
        for last in range(3):
            ans = (ans + sum(dp[last][offset+1:])) % MOD
        return ans

Implementation plan:

MOD = 10**9 + 7
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        n = len(s)
        mp = {'F':0,'W':1,'E':2}
        # inc[a][b]
        inc = [[0]*3 for _ in range(3)]
        for a in range(3):
            for b in range(3):
                if b == (a + 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = 1
                elif b == (a - 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = -1
        offset = n
        M = 2*n + 1
        dp = [[0]*M for _ in range(3)]
        a0 = mp[s[0]]
        for b in range(3):
            dp[b][offset + inc[a0][b]] = 1
        for ch in s[1:]:
            a = mp[ch]
            ndp = [[0]*M for _ in range(3)]
            for last in range(3):
                row = dp[last]
                # for b in range(3):
                for b in range(3):
                    if b == last:
                        continue
                    d = inc[a][b]
                    target_row = ndp[b]
                    if d == 0:
                        # But can d==0 when b==a. Since b != last, possible. Need update no shift.
                        for idx, val in enumerate(row):
                            if val:
                                nv = target_row[idx] + val
                                if nv >= MOD:
                                    nv -= MOD
                                target_row[idx] = nv
                    elif d == 1:
                        for idx in range(M-1):
                            val = row[idx]
                            if val:
                                nv = target_row[idx+1] + val
                                if nv >= MOD:
                                    nv -= MOD
                                target_row[idx+1] = nv
                    else: # d == -1
                        for idx in range(1, M):
                            val = row[idx]
                            if val:
                                nv = target_row[idx-1] + val
                                if nv >= MOD:
                                    nv -= MOD
                                target_row[idx-1] = nv
            dp = ndp
        ans = 0
        for last in range(3):
            ans = (ans + sum(dp[last][offset+1:])) % MOD
        return ans

But if we use enumerate(row) and val maybe 0, skip. Good.

Let's think about final answer code style. The problem says "Use the following starter code to write the final solution." So final code should include class Solution with method. We can include MOD inside method or class. Good.

Let's maybe write final code:

```python
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(s)
        mp = {'F': 0, 'W': 1, 'E': 2}
        
        inc = [[0] * 3 for _ in range(3)]
        for a in range(3):
            for b in range(3):
                if b == (a + 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = 1
                elif b == (a - 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = -1
        
        offset = n
        M = 2 * n + 1
        dp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]
        
        first = mp[s[0]]
        for b in range(3):
            dp[b][offset + inc[first][b]] = 1
        
        for i in range(1, n):
            a = mp[s[i]]
            ndp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]
            for last in range(3):
                row = dp[last]
                for b in range(3):
                    if b == last:
                        continue
                    d = inc[a][b]
                    target = ndp[b]
                    if d == 0:
                        start, stop = 0, M
                    elif d == 1:
                        start, stop = 0, M - 1
                    else:
                        start, stop = 1, M
                    for idx in range(start, stop):
                        val = row[idx]
                        if val:
                            nv = target[idx + d] + val
                            if nv >= MOD:
                                nv -= MOD
                            target[idx + d] = nv
            dp = ndp
        
        ans = 0
        for last in range(3):
            ans = (ans + sum(dp[last][offset + 1:])) % MOD
        return ans
```

Full code:

```python
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(s)
        mp = {'F': 0, 'W': 1, 'E': 2}
        
        inc = [[0] * 3 for _ in range(3)]
        for a in range(3):
            for b in range(3):
                if b == (a + 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = 1
                elif b == (a - 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = -1
        
        trans = []
        for a in range(3):
            by_last = []
            for last in range(3):
                by_last.append([(b, inc[a][b]) for b in range(3) if b != last])
            trans.append(by_last)
        
        offset = n
        M = 2 * n + 1
        dp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]
        
        first = mp[s[0]]
        for b in range(3):
            dp[b][offset + inc[first][b]] = 1
        
        for i in range(1, n):
            a = mp[s[i]]
            ndp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]
            for last in range(3):
                row = dp[last]
                for b, d in trans[a][last]:
                    target = ndp[b]
                    if d == 0:
                        start, stop = 0, M
                    elif d == 1:
                        start, stop = 0, M - 1
                    else:
                        start, stop = 1, M
                    for idx in range(start, stop):
                        val = row[idx]
                        if val:
                            nv = target[idx + d] + val
                            if nv >= MOD:
                                nv -= MOD
                            target[idx + d] = nv
            dp = ndp
        
        ans = 0
        for last in range(3):
            ans = (ans + sum(dp[last][offset + 1:])) % MOD
        return ans
```

Let's test with n=1: trans not used. dp init. ans. Good.

Let's think about if `trans[a][last]` includes b where b != last but d maybe -1,0,1. Good.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def countWinningSequences(self, s: str) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(s)
        mp = {'F': 0, 'W': 1, 'E': 2}

        inc = [[0] * 3 for _ in range(3)]
        for a in range(3):
            for b in range(3):
                if b == (a + 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = 1
                elif b == (a - 1) % 3:
                    inc[a][b] = -1

        trans = []
        for a in range(3):
            by_last = []
            for last in range(3):
                by_last.append([(b, inc[a][b]) for b in range(3) if b != last])
            trans.append(by_last)

        offset = n
        M = 2 * n + 1
        dp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]

        first = mp[s[0]]
        for b in range(3):
            dp[b][offset + inc[first][b]] = 1

        for i in range(1, n):
            a = mp[s[i]]
            ndp = [[0] * M for _ in range(3)]

            for last in range(3):
                row = dp[last]
                for b, d in trans[a][last]:
                    target = ndp[b]

                    if d == 0:
                        start, stop = 0, M
                    elif d == 1:
                        start, stop = 0, M - 1
                    else:
                        start, stop = 1, M

                    for idx in range(start, stop):
                        val = row[idx]
                        if val:
                            nv = target[idx + d] + val
                            if nv >= MOD:
                                nv -= MOD
                            target[idx + d] = nv

            dp = ndp

        ans = 0
        for last in range(3):
            ans = (ans + sum(dp[last][offset + 1:])) % MOD

        return ans
```
